3200:
3201:Your knowledge of Cloudflare Workers APIs, types, and configuration may be outdated. **Prefer retrieval over pre-training** for any Workers code task — writing or reviewing.
3202:
3203:## Retrieval Sources
3204:
3205:Fetch the **latest** versions before writing or reviewing Workers code. Do not rely on baked-in knowledge for API signatures, config fields, or binding shapes.
3206:
3207:| Source | How to retrieve | Use for |
3208:|--------|----------------|---------|
3209:| Workers best practices | Fetch `https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/best-practices/workers-best-practices/` | Canonical rules, patterns, anti-patterns |
3210:| Workers types | See `references/review.md` for retrieval steps | API signatures, handler types, binding types |
3211:| Wrangler config schema | `node_modules/wrangler/config-schema.json` | Config fields, binding shapes, allowed values |
3212:| Cloudflare docs | Search tool or `https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/` | API reference, compatibility dates/flags |
3213:
3214:## FIRST: Fetch Latest References
3215:
3216:Before reviewing or writing Workers code, retrieve the current best practices page and relevant type definitions. If the project's `node_modules` has an older version, **prefer the latest published version**.
3217:
3218:```bash
3219:# Fetch latest workers types
3220:mkdir -p /tmp/workers-types-latest && \
3221:  npm pack @cloudflare/workers-types --pack-destination /tmp/workers-types-latest && \
3222:  tar -xzf /tmp/workers-types-latest/cloudflare-workers-types-*.tgz -C /tmp/workers-types-latest
3223:# Types at /tmp/workers-types-latest/package/index.d.ts
3224:```
3225:
3226:## Reference Documentation
3227:
3228:- `references/rules.md` — all best practice rules with code examples and anti-patterns
3229:- `references/review.md` — type validation, config validation, binding access patterns, review process
3230:
3231:## Rules Quick Reference
3232:
3233:### Configuration
3234:
3235:| Rule | Summary |
3236:|------|---------|
3237:| Compatibility date | Set `compatibility_date` to today on new projects; update periodically on existing ones |
3238:| nodejs_compat | Enable the `nodejs_compat` flag — many libraries depend on Node.js built-ins |
3239:| wrangler types | Run `wrangler types` to generate `Env` — never hand-write binding interfaces |
3240:| Secrets | Use `wrangler secret put`, never hardcode secrets in config or source |
3241:| wrangler.jsonc | Use JSONC config for non-secret settings — newer features are JSON-only |
3242:
3243:### Request & Response Handling
3244:
3245:| Rule | Summary |
3246:|------|---------|
3247:| Streaming | Stream large/unknown payloads — never `await response.text()` on unbounded data |
3248:| waitUntil | Use `ctx.waitUntil()` for post-response work; do not destructure `ctx` |
3249:
3250:### Architecture
3251:
3252:| Rule | Summary |
3253:|------|---------|
3254:| Bindings over REST | Use in-process bindings (KV, R2, D1, Queues) — not the Cloudflare REST API |
3255:| Queues & Workflows | Move async/background work off the critical path |
3256:| Service bindings | Use service bindings for Worker-to-Worker calls — not public HTTP |
3257:| Hyperdrive | Always use Hyperdrive for external PostgreSQL/MySQL connections |
3258:
3259:### Observability
3260:
3261:| Rule | Summary |
3262:|------|---------|
3263:| Logs & Traces | Enable `observability` in config with `head_sampling_rate`; use structured JSON logging |
3264:
3265:### Code Patterns
3266:
3267:| Rule | Summary |
3268:|------|---------|
3269:| No global request state | Never store request-scoped data in module-level variables |
3270:| Floating promises | Every Promise must be `await`ed, `return`ed, `void`ed, or passed to `ctx.waitUntil()` |
3271:
3272:### Security
3273:
3274:| Rule | Summary |
3275:|------|---------|
3276:| Web Crypto | Use `crypto.randomUUID()` / `crypto.getRandomValues()` — never `Math.random()` for security |
3277:| No passThroughOnException | Use explicit try/catch with structured error responses |
3278:
3279:## Anti-Patterns to Flag
3280:
3281:| Anti-pattern | Why it matters |
3282:|-------------|----------------|
3283:| `await response.text()` on unbounded data | Memory exhaustion — 128 MB limit |
3284:| Hardcoded secrets in source or config | Credential leak via version control |
3285:| `Math.random()` for tokens/IDs | Predictable, not cryptographically secure |
3286:| Bare `fetch()` without `await` or `waitUntil` | Floating promise — dropped result, swallowed error |
3287:| Module-level mutable variables for request state | Cross-request data leaks, stale state, I/O errors |
3288:| Cloudflare REST API from inside a Worker | Unnecessary network hop, auth overhead, added latency |
3289:| `ctx.passThroughOnException()` as error handling | Hides bugs, makes debugging impossible |
3290:| Hand-written `Env` interface | Drifts from actual wrangler config bindings |
3291:| Direct string comparison for secret values | Timing side-channel — use `crypto.subtle.timingSafeEqual` |
3292:| Destructuring `ctx` (`const { waitUntil } = ctx`) | Loses `this` binding — throws "Illegal invocation" at runtime |
3293:| `any` on `Env` or handler params | Defeats type safety for all binding access |
3294:| `as unknown as T` double-cast | Hides real type incompatibilities — fix the design |
3295:| `implements` on platform base classes (instead of `extends`) | Legacy — loses `this.ctx`, `this.env`. Applies to DurableObject, WorkerEntrypoint, Workflow |
3296:| `env.X` inside platform base class | Should be `this.env.X` in classes extending DurableObject, WorkerEntrypoint, etc. |
3297:
3298:## Review Workflow
3299:
3300:1. **Retrieve** — fetch latest best practices page, workers types, and wrangler schema
3301:2. **Read full files** — not just diffs; context matters for binding access patterns
3302:3. **Check types** — binding access, handler signatures, no `any`, no unsafe casts (see `references/review.md`)
3303:4. **Check config** — compatibility_date, nodejs_compat, observability, secrets, binding-code consistency
3304:5. **Check patterns** — streaming, floating promises, global state, serialization boundaries
3305:6. **Check security** — crypto usage, secret handling, timing-safe comparisons, error handling
3306:7. **Validate with tools** — `npx tsc --noEmit`, lint for `no-floating-promises`
3307:8. **Reference rules** — see `references/rules.md` for each rule's correct pattern
3308:
3309:## Scope

3312:
3313:- **Durable Objects**: load the `durable-objects` skill
3314:- **Workflows**: see [Rules of Workflows](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows/build/rules-of-workflows/)

3317:## Principles
3318:
3319:- **Be certain.** Retrieve before flagging. If unsure about an API, config field, or pattern, fetch the docs first.
3320:- **Provide evidence.** Reference line numbers, tool output, or docs links.
3321:- **Focus on what developers will copy.** Workers code in examples and docs gets pasted into production.
3322:- **Correctness over completeness.** A concise example that works beats a comprehensive one with errors.

3324:FILE: C:\Users\ivych\.codex\skills\workers-best-practices\references\rules.md
3325:# Workers Best Practices — Rules
3326:
3327:Each rule has an imperative summary, what to check, the correct pattern, and an anti-pattern where applicable. Code examples are plain TypeScript — no MDX components.
3328:
3329:When a rule involves config fields or API signatures that may evolve, a **Retrieve** callout reminds you to check the latest docs or types before flagging. All doc paths are relative to `https://developers.cloudflare.com`.

3331:---
3332:
3333:## Configuration
3334:
3335:### Keep compatibility_date current
3336:
3337:Set `compatibility_date` to today on new projects. Update periodically on existing ones to access new APIs and fixes.
3338:
3339:**Check**: `compatibility_date` exists. Flag if older than 6 months.
3340:
3341:```jsonc
3342:// wrangler.jsonc
3343:{
3344:  "compatibility_date": "$today",  // Replace with today's date (YYYY-MM-DD)
3345:  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat"]
3346:}
3347:```
3348:
3349:**Retrieve**: current compatibility dates at `/workers/configuration/compatibility-dates/`.
3350:
3351:### Enable nodejs_compat
3352:
3353:The `nodejs_compat` flag enables Node.js built-in modules (`node:crypto`, `node:buffer`, `node:stream`). Many libraries require it. Missing this flag causes cryptic import errors at runtime.
3354:
3355:**Check**: `compatibility_flags` includes `"nodejs_compat"`.
3356:
3357:```jsonc
3358:{
3359:  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat"]
3360:}
3361:```
3362:
3363:### Generate binding types with wrangler types
3364:
3365:Never hand-write the `Env` interface. Run `wrangler types` to generate it from the wrangler config. Re-run after adding or renaming any binding.
3366:
3367:**Check**: no manually defined `Env` or `interface Env` that duplicates wrangler config bindings. Look for `satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>` pattern on the default export.
3368:
3369:```ts
3370:// Generated by wrangler types — always matches actual config
3371:export default {
3372:  async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
3373:    const value = await env.MY_KV.get("key");
3374:    return new Response(value);
3375:  },
3376:} satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>;
3377:```
3378:
3379:Anti-pattern:
3380:```ts
3381:// Hand-written Env that drifts from actual bindings
3382:interface Env {
3383:  MY_KV: KVNamespace;  // What if the binding name changed?
3384:}
3385:```
3386:
3387:### Store secrets with wrangler secret
3388:
3389:Secrets must never appear in wrangler config or source code. Use `wrangler secret put` and access via `env` at runtime. Non-secret config goes in `vars`.
3390:
3391:**Check**: no string literals that look like API keys, tokens, or credentials. Verify `.env` is in `.gitignore` for local dev.
3392:
3393:```jsonc
3394:{
3395:  "vars": {
3396:    "API_BASE_URL": "https://api.example.com"  // Non-secret: OK in config
3397:  }
3398:  // Secrets set via: wrangler secret put API_KEY
3399:}
3400:```
3401:
3402:Anti-pattern:
3403:```jsonc
3404:{
3405:  "vars": {
3406:    "API_KEY": "sk-live-abc123..."  // Secret in version control
3407:  }
3408:}
3409:```
3410:
3411:### Use wrangler.jsonc for config
3412:
3413:Prefer `wrangler.jsonc` over `wrangler.toml`. Newer features are JSON-only. JSONC supports comments for documenting config decisions.
3414:
3415:**Check**: project uses `wrangler.jsonc` (or `wrangler.json`). Flag `wrangler.toml` in new projects.
3416:
3417:---
3418:
3419:## Request & Response Handling
3420:
3421:### Stream request and response bodies
3422:
3423:Workers have a 128 MB memory limit. Buffering entire bodies with `await response.text()` or `await request.arrayBuffer()` crashes on large payloads. Stream data through using `TransformStream` or pass `response.body` directly.
3424:
3425:**Check**: any `await response.text()`, `await response.json()`, or `await response.arrayBuffer()` on data that could be large or unbounded. Small, bounded payloads (known-size JSON, config files) are fine to buffer.
3426:
3427:Correct — stream through:
3428:```ts
3429:async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
3430:  const response = await fetch("https://api.example.com/large-dataset");
3431:  return new Response(response.body, response);
3432:}
3433:```
3434:
3435:Correct — concatenate multiple streams:
3436:```ts
3437:async fetch(request: Request, env: Env, ctx: ExecutionContext): Promise<Response> {
3438:  const urls = ["https://api.example.com/part-1", "https://api.example.com/part-2"];
3439:  const { readable, writable } = new TransformStream();
3440:
3441:  // Track the pipeline promise — don't let it float
3442:  ctx.waitUntil((async () => {
3443:    for (const url of urls) {
3444:      const response = await fetch(url);
3445:      if (response.body) {
3446:        await response.body.pipeTo(writable, { preventClose: true });
3447:      }
3448:    }
3449:    await writable.close();
3450:  })());
3451:
3452:  return new Response(readable, {
3453:    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/octet-stream" },
3454:  });
3455:}
3456:```
3457:
3458:Anti-pattern:
3459:```ts
3460:// Buffers entire body — crashes on large payloads
3461:const response = await fetch("https://api.example.com/large-dataset");
3462:const text = await response.text();
3463:return new Response(text);
3464:```
3465:
3466:**Retrieve**: streaming APIs at `/workers/runtime-apis/streams/`.
3467:
3468:### Use waitUntil for work after the response
3469:
3470:`ctx.waitUntil()` performs background work (analytics, cache writes, webhooks) after the response is sent. Keeps response fast. 30-second time limit after response.
3471:
3472:**Check**: background work uses `ctx.waitUntil()`, not inline `await`. Do not destructure `ctx` — it loses the `this` binding and throws "Illegal invocation".
3473:
3474:```ts
3475:async fetch(request: Request, env: Env, ctx: ExecutionContext): Promise<Response> {
3476:  const data = await processRequest(request);
3477:
3478:  ctx.waitUntil(logToAnalytics(env, data));
3479:  ctx.waitUntil(updateCache(env, data));
3480:
3481:  return Response.json(data);
3482:}
3483:```
3484:
3485:Anti-pattern:
3486:```ts
3487:// Destructuring ctx loses the this binding
3488:const { waitUntil } = ctx;  // "Illegal invocation" at runtime
3489:waitUntil(somePromise);
3490:```
3491:
3492:---
3493:
3494:## Architecture
3495:
3496:### Use bindings for Cloudflare services, not REST APIs
3497:
3498:Bindings (KV, R2, D1, Queues, Workflows) are direct, in-process references — no network hop, no authentication, no extra latency. Using the Cloudflare REST API from a Worker wastes time and adds complexity.
3499:
3500:**Check**: no `fetch("https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/...")` calls for services available as bindings.
3501:
3502:```ts
3503:// Binding — direct, zero-cost
3504:const object = await env.MY_BUCKET.get("my-file");
3505:```
3506:
3507:Anti-pattern:
3508:```ts
3509:// REST API from inside a Worker — unnecessary overhead
3510:const response = await fetch(
3511:  "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/.../r2/buckets/.../objects/my-file",
3512:  { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${env.CF_API_TOKEN}` } }
3513:);
3514:```
3515:
3516:### Use Queues and Workflows for async and background work
3517:
3518:Long-running, retriable, or non-urgent tasks should not block a request.
3519:
3520:- **Queues**: decouple producer from consumer. Fan-out, buffering/batching, simple single-step background jobs. At-least-once delivery.
3521:- **Workflows**: multi-step durable execution. Each step's return value is persisted; only failed steps retry. Can run for hours/days/weeks.
3522:- **Both together**: Queue buffers high-throughput entry, consumer creates Workflow instances for complex processing.
3523:
3524:**Check**: long-running work (email sends, webhooks, multi-step processes) is offloaded to Queues or Workflows, not done inline in the fetch handler.
3525:
3526:```ts
3527:async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
3528:  const order = await request.json<{ id: string; type: string }>();
3529:
3530:  if (order.type === "simple") {
3531:    await env.ORDER_QUEUE.send({ orderId: order.id, action: "send-email" });
3532:  } else {
3533:    await env.FULFILLMENT_WORKFLOW.create({ params: { orderId: order.id } });
3534:  }
3535:
3536:  return Response.json({ status: "accepted" }, { status: 202 });
3537:}
3538:```
3539:
3540:**Retrieve**: `/queues/` and `/workflows/` for current APIs. For Workflow-specific rules, see [Rules of Workflows](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows/build/rules-of-workflows/).
3541:
3542:### Use service bindings for Worker-to-Worker communication
3543:
3544:Service bindings are zero-cost, bypass the public internet, and support type-safe RPC. Do not call another Worker via its public URL.
3545:
3546:**Check**: Worker-to-Worker calls use `env.SERVICE_NAME.method()` (RPC) or `env.SERVICE_NAME.fetch()`, not `fetch("https://my-other-worker.example.com/...")`.
3547:
3548:```ts
3549:import { WorkerEntrypoint } from "cloudflare:workers";
3550:
3551:export class AuthService extends WorkerEntrypoint {
3552:  async verifyToken(token: string): Promise<{ userId: string; valid: boolean }> {
3553:    return { userId: "user-123", valid: true };
3554:  }
3555:}
3556:
3557:// Caller Worker
3558:const auth = await env.AUTH_SERVICE.verifyToken(token);
3559:```
3560:
3561:**Retrieve**: verify `WorkerEntrypoint` import path and signature against latest `@cloudflare/workers-types`.
3562:
3563:### Use Hyperdrive for external database connections
3564:
3565:Hyperdrive maintains a regional connection pool, eliminating per-request TCP + TLS + auth cost (often 300-500ms). Create a new `Client` per request — Hyperdrive manages the underlying pool. Requires `nodejs_compat`.
3566:
3567:**Check**: any `new Client()` or database connection that uses a direct connection string instead of `env.HYPERDRIVE.connectionString`.
3568:
3569:```jsonc
3570:{
3571:  "hyperdrive": [{ "binding": "HYPERDRIVE", "id": "<YOUR_HYPERDRIVE_ID>" }]
3572:}
3573:```
3574:
3575:```ts
3576:import { Client } from "pg";
3577:
3578:async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
3579:  const client = new Client({ connectionString: env.HYPERDRIVE.connectionString });
3580:  await client.connect();
3581:  const result = await client.query("SELECT id, name FROM users LIMIT 10");
3582:  return Response.json(result.rows);
3583:}
3584:```
3585:
3586:**Retrieve**: `/hyperdrive/` for current configuration and supported databases.
3587:

3589:
3590:## Observability
3591:
3592:### Enable Workers Logs and Traces
3593:
3594:Enable `observability` in wrangler config before deploying to production. Use `head_sampling_rate` to control volume and cost. Use structured JSON logging — `console.log(JSON.stringify({...}))` — so logs are searchable. Use `console.error` for errors (appears at error severity in the dashboard).
3595:
3596:**Check**: `observability.enabled` is `true` in config. Logging uses structured JSON, not string concatenation.
3597:
3598:```jsonc
3599:{
3600:  "observability": {
3601:    "enabled": true,
3602:    "logs": { "head_sampling_rate": 1 },
3603:    "traces": { "enabled": true, "head_sampling_rate": 0.01 }
3604:  }
3605:}
3606:```
3607:
3608:```ts
3609:// Structured JSON — searchable and filterable
3610:console.log(JSON.stringify({ message: "incoming request", method: request.method, path: url.pathname }));
3611:
3612:// Error severity
3613:console.error(JSON.stringify({ message: "request failed", error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e) }));
3614:```
3615:
3616:Anti-pattern:
3617:```ts
3618:// Unstructured string logs — hard to query
3619:console.log("Got a request to " + url.pathname);
3620:```
3621:
3622:**Retrieve**: `/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/` and `/workers/observability/traces/` for current config options.
3623:
3624:---
3625:
3626:## Code Patterns
3627:
3628:### Do not store request-scoped state in global scope
3629:
3630:Workers reuse isolates across requests. Module-level mutable variables cause cross-request data leaks, stale state, and "Cannot perform I/O on behalf of a different request" errors.
3631:
3632:**Check**: no mutable `let`/`var` at module scope that gets assigned inside a handler. Pass state through function arguments.
3633:
3634:```ts
3635:export default {
3636:  async fetch(request: Request, env: Env, ctx: ExecutionContext): Promise<Response> {
3637:    const userId = request.headers.get("X-User-Id");
3638:    const result = await handleRequest(userId, env);
3639:    return Response.json(result);
3640:  },
3641:} satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>;
3642:```
3643:
3644:Anti-pattern:
3645:```ts
3646:// Module-level mutable state — leaks between requests
3647:let currentUser: string | null = null;
3648:
3649:export default {
3650:  async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
3651:    currentUser = request.headers.get("X-User-Id");  // Visible to next request
3652:    // ...
3653:  },
3654:};
3655:```
3656:
3657:### Always await or waitUntil Promises
3658:
3659:A Promise that is not `await`ed, `return`ed, or passed to `ctx.waitUntil()` is a floating promise. Causes: dropped results, swallowed errors, unfinished work. The runtime may terminate the isolate before it completes.
3660:
3661:**Check**: every `fetch()`, `env.*.put()`, `env.*.send()`, and any other async call is handled. Enable `no-floating-promises` lint rule.
3662:
3663:```bash
3664:# ESLint
3665:npx eslint --rule '{"@typescript-eslint/no-floating-promises": "error"}' src/
3666:
3667:# oxlint
3668:npx oxlint --deny typescript/no-floating-promises src/
3669:```
3670:
3671:```ts
3672:// Correct: await when you need the result
3673:const response = await fetch("https://api.example.com/process", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify(data) });
3674:
3675:// Correct: waitUntil when you don't need the result before responding
3676:ctx.waitUntil(fetch("https://api.example.com/webhook", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify(data) }));
3677:```
3678:
3679:Anti-pattern:
3680:```ts
3681:// Floating promise — result dropped, error swallowed
3682:fetch("https://api.example.com/webhook", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify(data) });
3683:```
3684:
3685:### Be aware of platform limits
3686:
3687:Workers have a 10ms CPU time limit (Bundled) or 30s (Standard/Unbound). Heavy synchronous work — tight loops, large JSON parsing, compute-intensive crypto — can hit the CPU limit and terminate the request.
3688:
3689:**Check**: compute-heavy operations that run synchronously. Consider breaking work into smaller chunks, offloading to Queues/Workflows, or using WebAssembly for CPU-intensive tasks.
3690:
3691:**Retrieve**: current limits at `/workers/platform/limits/`.
3692:
3693:---
3694:
3695:## Security
3696:
3697:### Use Web Crypto for secure token generation
3698:
3699:Use `crypto.randomUUID()` for unique IDs and `crypto.getRandomValues()` for random bytes. `Math.random()` is not cryptographically secure.
3700:
3701:For comparing secrets (API keys, HMAC signatures), use `crypto.subtle.timingSafeEqual()`. Hash both values to a fixed size first — do not short-circuit on length mismatch (leaks length via timing).
3702:
3703:**Check**: no `Math.random()` for security-sensitive values. Secret comparisons use `timingSafeEqual` with fixed-size hashing.
3704:
3705:```ts
3706:// Secure random UUID
3707:const sessionId = crypto.randomUUID();
3708:
3709:// Secure random bytes
3710:const tokenBytes = new Uint8Array(32);
3711:crypto.getRandomValues(tokenBytes);
3712:const token = Array.from(tokenBytes).map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")).join("");
3713:```
3714:
3715:```ts
3716:// Constant-time comparison — hash first to avoid length leak
3717:async function verifyToken(provided: string, expected: string): Promise<boolean> {
3718:  const encoder = new TextEncoder();
3719:  const [providedHash, expectedHash] = await Promise.all([
3720:    crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", encoder.encode(provided)),
3721:    crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", encoder.encode(expected)),
3722:  ]);
3723:  return crypto.subtle.timingSafeEqual(providedHash, expectedHash);
3724:}
3725:```
3726:
3727:Anti-pattern:
3728:```ts
3729:// Predictable — not cryptographically secure
3730:const token = Math.random().toString(36).substring(2);
3731:
3732:// Timing side-channel — leaks information about the expected value
3733:return provided === expected;
3734:```
3735:
3736:**Retrieve**: `/workers/runtime-apis/web-crypto/` for current API surface.
3737:
3738:### Explicit error handling over passThroughOnException
3739:
3740:`passThroughOnException()` is a fail-open mechanism that sends requests to the origin when the Worker throws. It hides bugs and makes debugging difficult. Use explicit try/catch with structured error responses.
3741:
3742:**Check**: no `ctx.passThroughOnException()` calls. Error handling uses try/catch with structured JSON error responses and `console.error`.
3743:
3744:```ts
3745:async fetch(request: Request, env: Env, ctx: ExecutionContext): Promise<Response> {
3746:  try {
3747:    const result = await handleRequest(request, env);
3748:    return Response.json(result);
3749:  } catch (error) {
3750:    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : "Unknown error";
3751:    console.error(JSON.stringify({ message: "unhandled error", error: message, path: new URL(request.url).pathname }));
3752:    return Response.json({ error: "Internal server error" }, { status: 500 });
3753:  }
3754:}
3755:```

3758:
3759:## Development & Testing
3760:
3761:### Test with @cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers
3762:
3763:Runs tests inside the Workers runtime with real bindings. Catches issues that Node.js-based tests miss.
3764:
3765:**Known pitfall**: the Vitest pool auto-injects `nodejs_compat`, so tests pass even if your wrangler config is missing the flag. Always confirm your `wrangler.jsonc` includes `nodejs_compat` if your code depends on Node.js built-ins.
3766:
3767:**Check**: test setup uses `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers`. Tests cover nullable returns (e.g., KV `.get()` returning `null`).
3768:
3769:```ts
3770:import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
3771:import { env } from "cloudflare:test";
3772:
3773:describe("KV operations", () => {
3774:  it("should store and retrieve a value", async () => {
3775:    await env.MY_KV.put("key", "value");
3776:    const result = await env.MY_KV.get("key");
3777:    expect(result).toBe("value");
3778:  });
3779:
3780:  it("should return null for missing keys", async () => {
3781:    const result = await env.MY_KV.get("nonexistent");
3782:    expect(result).toBeNull();
3783:  });
3784:});
3785:```
3786:
3787:**Retrieve**: `/workers/testing/vitest-integration/` for current setup and configuration.

3790:# Code Review — Workers
3791:
3792:How to review Workers code for type correctness, API usage, config validity, and best practices. This is self-contained — do not assume access to other skills.
3793:
3794:## Retrieval
3795:
3796:Prefer retrieval over pre-training. Types, config schemas, and APIs change with compatibility dates and new bindings.
3797:
3798:### Workers types
3799:
3800:Fetch the latest `@cloudflare/workers-types` before reviewing. The project may have an older version installed.
3801:
3802:```bash
3803:mkdir -p /tmp/workers-types-latest && \
3804:  npm pack @cloudflare/workers-types --pack-destination /tmp/workers-types-latest && \
3805:  tar -xzf /tmp/workers-types-latest/cloudflare-workers-types-*.tgz -C /tmp/workers-types-latest
3806:# Types are at /tmp/workers-types-latest/package/index.d.ts
3807:```
3808:
3809:Search this file for the specific type, class, or interface under review. Do not guess type names.
3810:
3811:Alternative: `npx wrangler types` generates a typed `Env` interface from the local wrangler config.
3812:
3813:Fallback: read `node_modules/@cloudflare/workers-types/index.d.ts`. Note the installed version.
3814:
3815:### Wrangler config schema
3816:
3817:The authoritative schema is bundled with wrangler as `config-schema.json` (JSON Schema draft-07).
3818:
3819:```bash
3820:# Read from local node_modules
3821:cat node_modules/wrangler/config-schema.json
3822:```
3823:
3824:Do not guess field names or structures — look them up.
3825:
3826:### Cloudflare docs
3827:
3828:Use the Cloudflare docs search tool if available, or fetch from `https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/`. The best practices page lives at `/workers/best-practices/workers-best-practices/`.

3831:
3832:## Type Validation
3833:
3834:### Env interface
3835:
3836:- Every binding must have a specific type. Flag `any`, `unknown`, `object`, or `Record<string, unknown>` on bindings.
3837:- Binding types that accept generic parameters (Durable Object namespaces, Queues, Service bindings for RPC) must include them. Read the type definition to confirm which types are generic.
3838:- Binding names must match the wrangler config exactly.
3839:- Prefer generated types from `wrangler types` over hand-written interfaces.
3840:
3841:### Handler and class signatures
3842:
3843:Verify against current type definitions — do not assume signatures are stable.
3844:
3845:- Correct import path (most Workers platform classes import from `"cloudflare:workers"`)
3846:- Generic type parameter on base classes (e.g., `DurableObject<Env>`)
3847:- Binding access pattern: `env.X` in module export handlers, `this.env.X` in classes extending platform base classes
3848:- `ExecutionContext` as the third param in module export handlers (needed for `ctx.waitUntil()`)
3849:- `fetch()` handlers must return `Promise<Response>`
3850:
3851:### Binding access — the most common error
3852:
3853:- **Module export handlers** (`fetch`, `scheduled`, `queue`, `email`): bindings via `env.X` parameter
3854:- **Platform base classes** (`WorkerEntrypoint`, `DurableObject`, `Workflow`, `Agent`): bindings via `this.env.X`
3855:
3856:Flag `env.X` inside a class extending a platform base class. Flag `this.env.X` inside a module export handler.
3857:
3858:### Type integrity rules
3859:
3860:| Rule | Detail |
3861:|------|--------|
3862:| No `any` | Never on binding types, handler params, or API responses |
3863:| No double-casting | `as unknown as T` hides real incompatibilities — fix the underlying design |
3864:| Justify suppressions | `@ts-ignore`/`@ts-expect-error` must include a comment explaining why |
3865:| Prefer `satisfies` | Use `satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>` over `as` — validates without widening |
3866:| Validate, do not assert | Schema or type guard for untyped data (JSON, parsed bodies), not `as` |
3867:
3868:### Stale class patterns
3869:
3870:Old patterns survive in codebases long after APIs change.
3871:
3872:- **`extends` vs `implements`**: platform classes use `extends`, not `implements`. The `implements` pattern is legacy and loses `this.ctx`, `this.env`.
3873:- **Import paths**: verify module specifiers match what types actually export. Common mistake: wrong path for `"cloudflare:workers"` vs `"cloudflare:workflows"`.
3874:- **Renamed properties**: e.g., `this.state` to `this.ctx` in Durable Objects. Search types to confirm.
3875:- **Constructor signatures**: base class constructors change. Verify expected parameters.
3876:
3877:---
3878:
3879:## Config Validation
3880:
3881:### Required fields
3882:
3883:For executable examples, verify: `name`, `compatibility_date`, `main`. Check the schema for current required fields.
3884:
3885:### Config format
3886:
3887:- **JSONC** (`wrangler.jsonc`) — preferred for new projects
3888:- **JSON** (`wrangler.json`) — valid but no comments
3889:- **TOML** (`wrangler.toml`) — legacy; acceptable in existing content, flag in new projects
3890:
3891:### Binding-code consistency
3892:
3893:1. Every `env.X` reference in code has a corresponding binding declaration in config
3894:2. Every binding in config is referenced in code (warn on unused)
3895:3. Names match exactly (case-sensitive)
3896:4. For Durable Objects: `class_name` matches the exported class name
3897:
3898:### Common config mistakes
3899:
3900:| Check | What to look for |
3901:|-------|-----------------|
3902:| Stale `compatibility_date` | Should be recent; use `$today` placeholder in docs |
3903:| Missing DO migrations | Every new DO class needs a migration entry |
3904:| Binding name mismatch | Config `binding`/`name` must match `env.X` in code |
3905:| Secrets in config | Never in `vars` — use `wrangler secret put` |
3906:| Wrong binding key | Verify top-level key name against the schema |
3907:| Missing entrypoint | `main` required for executable Workers |
3908:
3909:---
3910:
3911:## Anti-Patterns to Flag
3912:
3913:See the full anti-patterns table in `SKILL.md`. The type-specific ones to watch for during review:
3914:
3915:- **`any` on `Env` or handler params** — defeats type safety for all downstream binding access
3916:- **`as unknown as T`** — hides real type incompatibilities; fix the underlying design
3917:- **`@ts-ignore`/`@ts-expect-error` without explanation** — masks errors silently; require a justifying comment
3918:- **`implements` instead of `extends` on platform base classes** — legacy pattern; loses `this.ctx`, `this.env`
3919:- **`env.X` inside class body** — should be `this.env.X` in platform base classes
3920:- **`this.env.X` in module export handler** — should be `env.X` parameter
3921:- **Non-serializable values across boundaries** — `Response`, `Error` in step/queue compiles but fails at runtime
3922:
3923:---
3924:
3925:## Serialization Boundaries
3926:
3927:Data crossing these boundaries must be structured-clone serializable:
3928:
3929:- **Queue messages**: body passed to `.send()` or `.sendBatch()`
3930:- **Workflow step return values**: persisted to durable storage
3931:- **DO storage**: values in `storage.put()` or SQL
3932:- **`postMessage()`**: WebSocket messages
3933:
3934:Non-serializable types to flag: `Response`, `Request`, `Error`, functions, class instances with methods, `Map`/`Set`, `Symbol`.
3935:
3936:Valid: plain objects, arrays, strings, numbers, booleans, null, `ArrayBuffer`, `Date`.
3937:
3938:---
3939:
3940:## Review Process
3941:
3942:1. **Retrieve** — fetch latest workers types, wrangler schema, and best practices page
3943:2. **Read full files** — not just diffs; context matters for binding access patterns
3944:3. **Categorize code** — determines what to check:
3945:   - **Illustrative** (concept demo, comments for most logic): verify correct API names and realistic signatures
3946:   - **Demonstrative** (functional snippet, would work in context): verify syntax, correct APIs, correct binding access
3947:   - **Executable** (standalone, runs without modification): verify compiles, runs, includes imports and config
3948:4. **Check types** — binding access pattern, handler signatures, no `any`, no unsafe casts
3949:5. **Check config** — compatibility_date, nodejs_compat, observability, secrets, binding-code consistency
3950:6. **Check patterns** — streaming, floating promises, global state, serialization boundaries
3951:7. **Check security** — crypto usage, secret handling, timing-safe comparisons, error handling
3952:8. **Validate with tools** — `npx tsc --noEmit`, lint for `no-floating-promises`
3953:9. **Assess risk** — HIGH (auth, crypto, bindings), MEDIUM (business logic, config), LOW (style, comments)
3954:
3955:### Output format
3956:
3957:```
3958:**[SEVERITY]** Brief description
3959:`file.ts:42` — explanation with evidence
3960:Suggested fix: `code`
3961:```
3962:
3963:Severity: **CRITICAL** (security, data loss, crash) | **HIGH** (type error, wrong API, broken config) | **MEDIUM** (missing validation, edge case) | **LOW** (style, minor improvement)
3964:

513:    "https://api.line.me/oauth2/v2.1/verify?access_token=" + encodeURIComponent(accessToken),
514:    { method: "GET" }
515:  );

518:    throw err;
519:  }
520:  const verifyData = await verifyRes.json();
521:  if (verifyData.client_id !== channelId) {
522:    throw { message: "Token not for this channel", status: 401 };
523:  }
524:  const profRes = await fetch("https://api.line.me/v2/profile", {
525:    method: "GET",
526:    headers: { "Authorization": "Bearer " + accessToken }
527:  });
528:  if (!profRes.ok) {
529:    throw { message: "Cannot resolve user", status: 401 };
530:  }
531:  const profile = await profRes.json();
532:  return { userId: profile.userId, displayName: profile.displayName || "" };

534:async function airtableFetch(apiKey, base, table, opts) {
535:  const params = new URLSearchParams();
536:  if (opts.filterByFormula) params.set("filterByFormula", opts.filterByFormula);
537:  if (opts.maxRecords) params.set("maxRecords", String(opts.maxRecords));
538:  if (opts.fields) opts.fields.forEach((f) => params.append("fields[]", f));
539:  if (opts.sort) opts.sort.forEach((s, i) => {
540:    params.set(`sort[${i}][field]`, s.field);
541:    params.set(`sort[${i}][direction]`, s.direction);
542:  });
543:  const res = await fetch(
544:    `https://api.airtable.com/v0/${base}/${table}?${params.toString()}`,
545:    { headers: { "Authorization": "Bearer " + apiKey } }
546:  );
547:  if (!res.ok) {
548:    console.log("Airtable error", res.status, await res.text());
549:    return { records: [] };
550:  }
551:  return res.json();

對 `resolveUserId` 不採納答辯：同意。這是既有共用函式的系統性錯誤分類，沒有造成 E25 新的授權繞過；本卡保留 `e.status/e.message` 傳遞並另立派工卡處理合理，不再列為 E25 BLOCKER。

但目前仍不能 APPROVED。

VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED

- [BLOCKER] 4096-byte 上限仍是「讀完後檢查」。計畫與實作先 `await request.text()`，再計算 bytes；大型 body 已完整進入記憶體。我以 12×1024-byte chunked stream 實測，回 413 前仍讀完全部 12 chunks。Cloudflare 明確要求在讀取前限制大小或串流限額。[計畫](/C:/Users/ivych/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--CBD-Lab-OS/80c6e79d-029f-4edf-bc51-22860d78f35e/scratchpad/mingge-line/plans/plan_e25_stamp_v0_1.md:48)、[實作](/C:/Users/ivych/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--CBD-Lab-OS/80c6e79d-029f-4edf-bc51-22860d78f35e/scratchpad/mingge-relay/worker.js:227)、[Cloudflare Workers best practices](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/best-practices/workers-best-practices/)。須補：先檢查 `Content-Length`，並用 `request.body.getReader()` 累計至 4097 bytes 即 cancel／413，兼顧無 `Content-Length` 的 chunked body。

- [BLOCKER] V3 仍未驗證實際成功／失敗轉態。它只執行 `buildStampSectionHtml`，沒有執行 `renderStampResult` 或 `submitStamp`。[測試](/C:/Users/ivych/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--CBD-Lab-OS/80c6e79d-029f-4edf-bc51-22860d78f35e/scratchpad/mingge-line/tests/test_e25_stamp_v1_0.sh:329)。須加入成功、fetch reject、非 200、壞 response contract 四條，驗證入口保留、按鈕重新啟用及常駐態只在成功後出現。

- [BLOCKER] `/history` 新欄位沒有回歸測試。目前只有 `/log` passthrough case。[測試](/C:/Users/ivych/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--CBD-Lab-OS/80c6e79d-029f-4edf-bc51-22860d78f35e/scratchpad/mingge-line/tests/test_e25_stamp_v1_0.sh:251)。須另測 `/history.records[]` 的 `trace_text`、`trace_at`。

- [BLOCKER] 44/44 驗證的 Worker artifact 身分未鎖定。測試預設載入 repo 外的 `../../mingge-relay/worker.js`，[路徑設定](/C:/Users/ivych/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--CBD-Lab-OS/80c6e79d-029f-4edf-bc51-22860d78f35e/scratchpad/mingge-line/tests/test_e25_stamp_v1_0.sh:13)；workspace 內 `workers/mingge-relay/worker.js` 目前沒有 `/trace`。須在計畫寫死 canonical 交付路徑或 SHA-256，並讓測試只驗證該檔，避免測過 A、部署 B。

- [BLOCKER] E2E 狀態文字互相矛盾：§5 說 Tier 2 本輪未執行，[邊界卻說已納入本輪機讀驗證](/C:/Users/ivych/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--CBD-Lab-OS/80c6e79d-029f-4edf-bc51-22860d78f35e/scratchpad/mingge-line/plans/plan_e25_stamp_v0_1.md:101)。後者須改成「納入 Perth 授權後 Tier 2，本輪未執行」。

其餘已採納項目方向可接受；Worker metadata 問題也可因交付範圍縮為 code artifact 而不阻擋，但不代表部署已獲核准。
